- **Direction**: The larger entity (Los Angeles) is described as enveloping the smaller one (Beverly Hills), so the predicate is A→B: Los Angeles contains Beverly Hills.  
- **Geometry**: The statement is about full enclosure, implying the Beverly Hills polygon lies entirely within the Los Angeles polygon.  
Suggested predicate: **contains**

BRANCH 2: Geometry‑first approach  
Using OSM data, the Los Angeles polygon is a large polygon covering the Greater Los Angeles area, while the Beverly Hills polygon is a small, distinct polygon entirely inside Los Angeles. A spatial overlay confirms that every point of Beverly Hills lies within the Los Angeles boundary, with no shared boundary or overlap.  
- **Equals check**: The bounding boxes and centroids differ; the polygons are not identical.  
- **Within/Contains**: Beverly Hills is fully inside Los Angeles, so Los Angeles contains Beverly Hills.

However, the Los Angeles bbox fully contains the Beverly Hills bbox, which is consistent with the textual claim of “completely enveloping.”  
- **Equals check**: Bbox sizes differ, so equals is out.  
- **Within/Contains**: Bbox containment suggests Los Angeles could contain Beverly Hills, but this is only a hint; actual polygon geometry would be needed for confirmation.  
- **Direction**: Los Angeles is the larger bbox, so if containment holds, it would be Los Angeles contains Beverly Hills.
